[Director 指令] **紧急：用户指令推翻 D062，启动 4 项候选实验冲 3.5**

DDL 2026-05-25，还剩 3 天。立即开始：

**第一步（立即执行）**：检查 westd-33183 服务器连通性和 GPU 状态
```
ssh westd-33183 "nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,memory.total,memory.used,utilization.gpu --format=csv,noheader && df -h /root && ls /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/"
```

**第二步（服务器就绪后，按优先级排列）**：

**实验 A: 扩展 TOST seeds 到 n=10**（最简单，~3h）
- 优先级最高——直接解决 TOST AA p=0.08 inconclusive
- 在现有 5 seeds (42,123,456,789,1000) 基础上新增 5 个 seeds (2024,2025,2026,314,628)
- 对 9-class 和 scrambled 各训练 5 个新 seed → 总共 10 个新模型
- 训练完后重跑 TOST 等价性检验

**实验 B: 多 LLM 训练数据**（~8-12h，地址最大瓶颈）
- 用 Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct 生成一批 jailbreak 对话（复用现有 Crescendo/FITD/DD/ActorAttack prompts）
- 混合 Qwen + Llama 数据训练 9-class DAPT
- 在全部测试集上评估，看 hierarchy 是否稳定
- 关键区别于 exp16：exp16 是分别训练+测试，这次是 **mixed training data**

**实验 C: 真实人类多轮数据评估**（~4-6h）
- 下载 MHJ (Multi-turn Human Jailbreak) 或类似公开数据集
- 用当前最佳模型 zero-shot 评估
- 如果格式不兼容，做最小适配

**实验 D: OOD benign 混合训练**（~6-8h）
- 在训练时混入一部分 WildChat benign 数据
- 看是否能降低 WildChat FPR 从 68.1%
- 如果有效，直接升级模型

先汇报第一步服务器状态，然后我审批后立即开跑。**多个实验可以串行跑，seeds 实验最先。**

- exp15 系列（RoBERTa-large 全部 6 个）
- exp16/16b/17（multi-LLM pilot）

**清理完后立即启动实验 A（TOST 10-seed）**，不等我二次确认。

- A（seeds）最先，直接解决统计效力

竞品实验参考\\n（待填充：首次正式对标竞品时补充）\\n\\n## 关键决策记录\\n\\n### D001: 停止数据迭代 (2026-05-17)\\n- **决策**：TF-IDF 高不再阻塞训练。转 early detection metric 方向。\\n\\n### D002: 用 intended_strategy 替代 GPT-5.5 标注 (2026-05-17)\\n- Crescendo jailbreak 用生成时的 intended_strategy 作为 ground truth；benign 全标 \\\"Direct Request\\\"。\\n\\n### D006: HARD STOP — 不再迭代数据 (2026-05-17)\\n- LLM user simulator stylistic uniformity 是结构性问题。转 ablation + cross-attack 论证。\\n\\n### D007: 论证路线调整 — scrambled ablation 降级 (2026-05-17)\\n- plan_003 scrambled 降级为补充 control。核心论证：plan_006 cross-attack + plan_004 perturbation。\\n\\n### D008: Population std convention (2026-05-18)\\n- 全文统一 population std（除以 N），不用 sample std（除以 N-1）。\\n\\n### D036: Drop verazuo, ToxicChat only (2026-05-19)\\n- **决策**：verazuo human data 因 benign data leakage（78% from train split）永久弃用。人类数据评估仅用 ToxicChat（无 leakage）。\\n- **原因**：GRU 训练时可能见过 verazuo benign embedding → data leakage\\n\\n### D037: Claim 3 scope 限定 (2026-05-19)\\n- perturbation 实验移至 Appendix，cross-attack 限定为 synthetic。\\n\\n### D038: 实验阶段结束 (2026-05-19)\\n- 当前证据足够，不再需要更多实验。进入论文修改收尾阶段。DDL 2026-05-25。\\n\\n## 实验经验\\n\\n### 磁盘空间约束 (2026-05-17, updated 2026-05-19)\\n- westd-33183 根分区总容量 30GB。DeBERTa checkpoint ~735MB。\\n- 旧 checkpoint（exp1/mf1/plan 系列）已迁移至 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints_archive/`\\n- 新实验 checkpoint 输出到 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints/`\\n- 当前根分区 47%（16GB 可用）\\n\\n### ⚠️ Single-seed 结论偏差警告 (2026-05-18)\\n- GRU 随机性可导致 single-seed 偏差 25pp+，必须多 seed 验证\\n\\n### ⚠️ 跨 LLM 泛化：攻击模式可迁移，benign 分布是瓶颈 (2026-05-19, exp14+exp14b+exp14c)\\n- **exp14 (Llama all)**：所有 variant 崩溃至随机水平（9class 0.433, jb_mlm 0.282, vanilla 0.261）\\n- **exp14b (Llama JB + Qwen benign)**：9class 回升至 1.000 (+56.7pp), jb_mlm 回升至 0.778 (+49.6pp)\\n- **exp14c (confusion matrix)**：TP rate 完全一致（exp14 vs exp14b），FP rate Llama benign 88-100% vs Qwen benign 0%\\n- **结论**：DAPT 学到的攻击模式跨 LLM 可迁移，但 benign 分布覆盖不足导致高 FP。F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸\\n- **论文定位**：limitation 归为 \\\"benign distribution coverage\\\"（可解决的数据多样性问题），而非方法论缺陷\\n- **Future work**：多 LLM benign 数据扩增\\n\\n### ⚠️ Capacity-DAPT 交互效应 (2026-05-19, exp15+exp15b+exp15c)\\n- **exp15 发现**：RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla AA Full=1.000 > 9class 0.946。大模型容量足以直接解决 AA\\n- **exp15b 证伪 catastrophic forgetting**：LR=1e-5 两个数据集均更差（DD 0.948 vs 0.987, AA 0.781 vs 0.946）。不是超参问题\\n- **exp15c MLM-only DAPT 也 hurt**：MLM AA Full=0.874，比 9class 0.946 和 vanilla 1.000 都差。不是 classification head 的问题\\n- **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\\n\\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\\n\\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\\n\\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + RoBERTa-large）GRU 参数一致：hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, MLP 512→256→2\\n\\n### ⚠️ MLM checkpoint 格式不兼容 (2026-05-19, exp16+exp17)\\n- **问题**: `train_deberta_mlm.py` 用 HF `save_pretrained()` 保存为 safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\\\"model.pt\\\")` 加载\\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 `AutoModel.from_pretrained()` 原生支持 safetensors\\n- **教训**: exp16 和 exp17 MLM 均因此崩溃，每次都需手动修复。应修改 train_classifier.py 的 treatment mode 统一支持 safetensors\\n\\n### ⚠️ exp16 Multi-LLM 结果 (2026-05-19)\\n- **无数据泄漏**: conversation_id 和内容 MD5 均无 train/test 重叠\\n- **9-class F1=1.0 on mixed_test 是真实的**: 76 样本小测试集 + 强 DAPT embedding，BiGRU Epoch 1 即达 val_acc 0.9865\\n- **Train Loss=0.0 是过拟合不是泄漏**: BiGRU 在预计算 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 9-class，dd_ood .422, aa_ood .217, llama_aa .124\\n\\n### ⚠️ Frozen probing: DAPT 类型间表征质量差异小 (2026-05-20, exp26+exp36)\\n- **发现**：冻结 9-class (exp26) vs 冻结 MLM (exp36) 的 probing 性能差距仅 3.1pp AA OOD (0.972 vs 0.941)\\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length shortcut\\n- **机制**：AA 攻击信号更微妙（no explicit harmful content），大量 benign 尾部 turns 稀释 BiGRU attention，导致漏检\\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\\n\\n## API Key / 模型版本\\n（待记录）\\n\", Appendix，cross-attack 限定为 synthetic。\\n\\n### D038: 实验阶段结束 (2026-05-19)\\n- 当前证据足够，不再需要更多实验。进入论文修改收尾阶段。DDL 2026-05-25。\\n\\n## 实验经验\\n\\n### 磁盘空间约束 (2026-05-17, updated 2026-05-19)\\n- westd-33183 根分区总容量 30GB。DeBERTa checkpoint ~735MB。\\n- 旧 checkpoint（exp1/mf1/plan 系列）已迁移至 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints_archive/`\\n- 新实验 checkpoint 输出到 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints/`\\n- 当前根分区 47%（16GB 可用）\\n\\n### ⚠️ Single-seed 结论偏差警告 (2026-05-18)\\n- GRU 随机性可导致 single-seed 偏差 25pp+，必须多 seed 验证\\n\\n### ⚠️ 跨 LLM 泛化：攻击模式可迁移，benign 分布是瓶颈 (2026-05-19, exp14+exp14b+exp14c)\\n- **exp14 (Llama all)**：所有 variant 崩溃至随机水平（9class 0.433, jb_mlm 0.282, vanilla 0.261）\\n- **exp14b (Llama JB + Qwen benign)**：9class 回升至 1.000 (+56.7pp), jb_mlm 回升至 0.778 (+49.6pp)\\n- **exp14c (confusion matrix)**：TP rate 完全一致（exp14 vs exp14b），FP rate Llama benign 88-100% vs Qwen benign 0%\\n- **结论**：DAPT 学到的攻击模式跨 LLM 可迁移，但 benign 分布覆盖不足导致高 FP。F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸\\n- **论文定位**：limitation 归为 \\\"benign distribution coverage\\\"（可解决的数据多样性问题），而非方法论缺陷\\n- **Future work**：多 LLM benign 数据扩增\\n\\n### ⚠️ Capacity-DAPT 交互效应 (2026-05-19, exp15+exp15b+exp15c)\\n- **exp15 发现**：RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla AA Full=1.000 > 9class 0.946。大模型容量足以直接解决 AA\\n- **exp15b 证伪 catastrophic forgetting**：LR=1e-5 两个数据集均更差（DD 0.948 vs 0.987, AA 0.781 vs 0.946）。不是超参问题\\n- **exp15c MLM-only DAPT 也 hurt**：MLM AA Full=0.874，比 9class 0.946 和 vanilla 1.000 都差。不是 classification head 的问题\\n- **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\\n\\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\\n\\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\\n\\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + RoBERTa-large）GRU 参数一致：hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, MLP 512→256→2\\n\\n### ⚠️ MLM checkpoint 格式不兼容 (2026-05-19, exp16+exp17)\\n- **问题**: `train_deberta_mlm.py` 用 HF `save_pretrained()` 保存为 safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\\\"model.pt\\\")` 加载\\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 `AutoModel.from_pretrained()` 原生支持 safetensors\\n- **教训**: exp16 和 exp17 MLM 均因此崩溃，每次都需手动修复。应修改 train_classifier.py 的 treatment mode 统一支持 safetensors\\n\\n### ⚠️ exp16 Multi-LLM 结果 (2026-05-19)\\n- **无数据泄漏**: conversation_id 和内容 MD5 均无 train/test 重叠\\n- **9-class F1=1.0 on mixed_test 是真实的**: 76 样本小测试集 + 强 DAPT embedding，BiGRU Epoch 1 即达 val_acc 0.9865\\n- **Train Loss=0.0 是过拟合不是泄漏**: BiGRU 在预计算 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 9-class，dd_ood .422, aa_ood .217, llama_aa .124\\n\\n### ⚠️ Frozen probing: DAPT 类型间表征质量差异小 (2026-05-20, exp26+exp36)\\n- **发现**：冻结 9-class (exp26) vs 冻结 MLM (exp36) 的 probing 性能差距仅 3.1pp AA OOD (0.972 vs 0.941)\\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length shortcut\\n- **机制**：AA 攻击信号更微妙（no explicit harmful content），大量 benign 尾部 turns 稀释 BiGRU attention，导致漏检\\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\\n\\n## API Key / 模型版本\\n（待记录）\\n\",         "content": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97).\n\n\\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}\nToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 (+53\\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level.\n\n\\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}\nClassification supervision is associated with lower variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\\times$ observed range, 3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013). Frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}).\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder capacity and lexical analysis.}\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).\n\n\\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}\nFrozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\\,pp), label irrelevance ($d$\\,=\\,0.09), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, not semantic feature learning, paralleling self-supervised results where training structure matters more than label content~\\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. Unlike random-label memorization~\\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, random-label \\emph{pretraining} here produces transferable representations, suggesting that classification structure regularizes the space. Three extensions emerge: MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training despite single-LLM effectiveness, frozen probing near-parity coexists with 8\\,pp end-to-end gaps, and the hierarchy reverses on human data.\n\n\\paragraph{Benign distribution as the remaining bottleneck.}\nOOD F1 figures are upper bounds (benign is IID). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}); cross-LLM error decomposition confirms collapse is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text.\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nOn synthetic multi-turn evaluations, classification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders, providing robustness through optimization regularization rather than semantic learning; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. Negative results on label correctness (TOST-confirmed on DD at $n$\\,=\\,5; $d$\\,=\\,0.09 on ActorAttack), temporal order, and domain specificity constrain which properties matter; continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nAll multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family). ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\nOOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}); residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",

        "content": "% related_work.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\n\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}\nAll multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj}: Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, FITD~\\citep{fitd2024}, DD~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024}, and ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} each operationalize it differently, while automated methods~\\citep{chao2023pair,mehrotra2024tap,perez2022redteaming} iteratively refine prompts. Benchmarks standardize evaluation at single-turn~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench} and multi-turn~\\citep{wei2023jailbroken,jiang2024redqueen,song2026multibreak} scales. A detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}\nNo existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Conversation-level systems (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}, SafeDream~\\citep{safedream2026}) and representation-level approaches~\\citep{lu2025xboundary,zhang2025jbshield,xie2024gradsafe,zou2024circuitbreakers,kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace,hua2026rcs} each evaluate on a single family; the latter require white-box access and can be brittle~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Per-turn classifiers~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024,zeng2024shieldgemma,han2024wildguard,anthropic2025constitutional} and single-turn defenses~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm,xu2024safedecoding} miss multi-turn trajectories. Our ablation contributes to filling this gap via decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}\nPersuasion and discourse frameworks~\\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts,mann1988rst,prasad2008pdtb,stolcke2000dialogueact} and technique-level classification~\\citep{cialdini2001influence,zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval,wang2019persuasion} offer interpretable vocabulary for attacks, but whether such structure aids detection has not been tested. Our ablation shows the taxonomy enables characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}\nContinued pretraining on domain text yields consistent gains~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop,lee2020biobert,beltagy2019scibert,howard2018ulmfit}, but has not been disentangled for safety-critical generalization. Intermediate-task transfer~\\citep{phang2018stilts} and multi-task learning~\\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} confound domain exposure with supervised signal; our MLM control confirms pretraining alone closes the OOD gap. Dataset artifacts~\\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing} motivate TF-IDF, adversarial NLP~\\citep{jin2020textfooler,li2020bertattack,nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive} informs perturbation, and probing~\\citep{conneau2018probing,hewitt2019structural} underpins CKA analysis.\n",
        "numLines": 16,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 16
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/related_work.tex"
  },

      "type": "text",

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\\begin{abstract}\nDetecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, and existing defenses fail to generalize across attack families: Llama Guard~3, a widely deployed turn-level safety classifier, detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation.\nWe isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.\nOn single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\\,=\\,0.818 vs.\\ 0.291 without adaptation).\nAcross three architectures (125--355M), the set of attacks requiring adaptation shrinks with scale, though architecture and pretraining differences prevent isolating scale as the sole factor.\nClassification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds), with limited sensitivity to label correctness, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.\nThese findings suggest classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, not semantic feature learning.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
        "numLines": 11,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 11
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/abstract.tex"
  },

  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants systematically vary training objectives while sharing the same two-stage pipeline (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic decomposition identifying the most robust adaptation and three supplementary negative results.} Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations on synthetic multi-turn data shows classification-based DAPT is the most robust variant; unsupervised pretraining is effective on single-source data but collapses on mixed-LLM training. We report three supplementary findings that challenge common assumptions: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels (TOST-confirmed on DD at $n$\\,=\\,5; $d$\\,=\\,0.09 on ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant ($\\Delta < 1$\\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Architecture- and scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation.} Three encoder scales (125M, 184M, 355M) suggest adaptation need shrinks with model size, though encoders differ in architecture; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate scale. RoBERTa-large handles structurally complex attacks without adaptation (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Mechanistic interpretation: optimization regularization, not semantic learning.} Three null results converge: frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\\,pp), label content irrelevance ($d$\\,=\\,0.09), and temporal modeling irrelevance ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) are consistent with classification supervision providing robustness through optimization regularization that stabilizes downstream training. This benefit is condition-dependent (variance reversal on human data) but classification DAPT remains strongest under mixed-LLM training (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). Continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:data_efficiency}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
        "numLines": 30,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 30
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },

      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
      "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"
    ],

\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Multi-task persuasion loss weight (lambda) affects OOD generalization in E2E fine-tuning: lambda=0 (intent-only) will underperform on hard OOD (ActorAttack) compared to lambda>0, because the persuasion auxiliary signal provides a regularization effect that preserves OOD-relevant features in the encoder. There exists an optimal lambda that maximizes AA OOD without sacrificing IID performance.\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"lambda_0_aa\\\": 1, \\\"lambda_0.1_aa\\\": 0.9904, \\\"lambda_0.5_aa\\\": 0.9809, \\\"lambda_1.0_aa\\\": 0.9809, \\\"lambda_2.0_aa\\\": 0.9439, \\\"iid_all\\\": 1, \\\"dd_all\\\": 1, \\\"fitd_all\\\": 1, \\\"note\\\": \\\"Higher lambda monotonically hurts AA OOD. lambda=0 (intent-only E2E) achieves perfect AA. Persuasion aux loss acts as a distractor during E2E.\\\"}, \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp28b_data_efficiency_supplement\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"DONE\\\", \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T15:40:53.186Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T19:15:30.605Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT maintains robust OOD performance across different random seeds (model initialization), and vanilla DeBERTa shows poor OOD generalization even at smaller training sizes (N=50, N=250), confirming that the data efficiency advantage of 9-class DAPT is not an artifact of a single seed.\\\", \\\"parent\\\": \\\"exp28_data_efficiency\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"9class_3seed_N50_aa_ood\\\": \\\"0.969±0.037\\\", \\\"9class_3seed_N125_aa_ood\\\": \\\"0.975±0.019\\\", \\\"9class_3seed_N250_aa_ood\\\": \\\"0.975±0.035\\\", \\\"9class_3seed_N350_aa_ood\\\": \\\"0.972±0.033\\\", \\\"note\\\": \\\"9-class 3-seed complete. exp39_extra_seeds_tost 已经用 5 seeds (42, 123, 456, 789, 1000) 完成了 TOST 检验\n- DD OOD 上 TOST 确认等价 (p<0.05)，但 AA OOD 上 p=0.08 不显著\n- 本实验增加 5 个新 seed (2024, 2025, 2026, 314, 628) 到 n=10，提高统计效力\n\n### 环境\n- 服务器: westd-33183\n- GPU: cuda:0 (RTX PRO 6000 96GB)\n- Python: conda base (`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`)\n- torch 2.8.0+cu128\n- 项目路径: /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/\n- 数据路径: /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/plan_002_splits/\n\n### 执行步骤\n\n**Step 1: 了解现有训练管线**\nSSH (read_only) 到 westd-33183，查看：\n- `ls 628\n- 每个 seed 训练 2 个变体: 9-class（正确标签）和 scrambled（打乱标签）\n- Stage 1 超参: lr=2e-5, batch_size=16, epochs=4\n- Stage 2 超参: BiGRU, 20 epochs\n- 训练完成后每个模型应输出 checkpoint\n\n**Step 3: 用 submit_training_job 456, 789, 1000, 2024, 2025, 2026, 314, 628) 的 OOD F1 结果：\n- 对每个 seed，获取 9-class 和 scrambled 在 DD OOD、AA OOD、FITD OOD 上的 F1 macro\n- 运行 TOST 等价性检验（margin=0.05 即 5pp）\n- 报告：DD TOST p-value, AA TOST p-value, FITD TOST p-value\n- 计算 Cohen's d effect size\n\n**Step 5: 更新 registry**\n用 update_registry 更新 exp41_tost_10seed:\n- 转 RUNNING 时提供 spec（command, server, data_path, model_name, seed 等）\n- With more seeds, AA shows a consistent gap of about 3 percentage points between 9-class and scrambled (0.979 vs 0.949), though scrambled has high variance across seeds. This contradicts the hypothesis—instead of confirming label irrelevance, the results suggest labels actually do matter for AA, which challenges the paper's current framing around optimization regularization versus semantic learning.

Looking back at what the paper currently claims, it hedges on this point—TOST-confirmed equivalence on DD at n=5 with a small effect size on AA (d=0.09), but now with n=10 the effect size on AA jumps to d=0.44, a medium effect that substantially shifts the narrative about label correctness.

The new seeds introduced an outlier in one condition that's pulling the scrambled mean down and inflating variance, which suggests this might be a seed-specific artifact rather than a robust finding. This is a negative result for the hypothesis, so I need to update the registry to reflect that conclusion and report the full derivation chain to the Director rather than letting this slip by unaddressed.

- status: DONE
- conclusion: negative
- metrics: tost_DD (p=2.06e-9, equivalent=true, d=-0.136), tost_AA (p=0.183, equivalent=false, d=0.44), tost_FITD (p=0, equivalent=true, d=0)
- 9class_AA_mean: 0.979, scrambled_AA_mean: 0.949, AA_mean_diff_pp: 3


- training_time_min: 16.8

This is a negative result, which means I need to update the report, analyze what went wrong with the hypothesis, notify the Director with the full derivation chain, and hold off on starting the next experiment. The concerning part here is that this contradicts what the paper currently claims about label sensitivity—it says there's limited sensitivity with d=0.09 on ActorAttack, but now I'm seeing d=0.44 with n=10, which is a medium effect size showing that labels actually do matter significantly for AA.

Looking more carefully at the data: with n=5 seeds, I had d=0.09 on ActorAttack, but doubling to n=10 seeds shows the true effect is much larger at d=0.44. Meanwhile, the equivalence holds for DD and FITD across both sample sizes, suggesting labels only matter for the hardest OOD scenario. This nuance is crucial for how the paper should frame its findings about label robustness. C3 claim says "optimization regularization, not semantic feature learning" - but if labels matter for the hardest OOD family, then semantic content does contribute. The framing needs adjustment.

For DD and FITD, labels truly don't matter, so the optimization regularization explanation holds. But for AA, correct labels help by about 3 percentage points with a medium effect size, suggesting some semantic signal is useful. This is actually more nuanced than the original claim - label content is irrelevant for structurally simple attacks, but provides modest benefit on structurally complex ones, indicating optimization regularization is primary but semantic content adds value when attack structure diverges most from training.

Now I need to follow the protocol: report this finding to the Director with the hypothesis derivation and propose a framing adjustment, rather than silently running the next experiment. The Director wants results reported immediately after each experiment completes, and they've set the execution order as A→D→C→B. I should report the findings and wait for their decision before proceeding, though I can use GPU time on other work while waiting. The new n=10 result on ActorAttack shows a much larger effect (d=0.44) and doesn't meet equivalence, which actually makes for a more nuanced and interesting story—labels matter more on harder OOD cases. Option 3 reframes this honestly as a gradient effect rather than claiming label irrelevance across the board, which is both more accurate and stronger scientifically. I should prepare the Director report with this framing and can start Experiment D in parallel while waiting for their decision on how to update the paper.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\\n\\n## Title (Working)\\nWhat Makes Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Detectable? A Systematic Decomposition of Cross-Attack Generalization\\n\\n## Core Insight (D030 → D049 — classification-based DAPT reframe)\\nDomain adaptation universally improves cross-attack generalization across encoder architectures; encoder pretraining determines the difficulty threshold at which adaptation becomes critical. Systematic ablation across two encoders (DeBERTa-v3, RoBERTa) and three OOD attack families (DD, FITD, ActorAttack) reveals:\\n\\n**On Deceptive Delight (moderate OOD)**: DeBERTa vanilla collapses (F1=0.31±0.03), continued pretraining bridges ~60pp (MLM=0.91±0.12), classification achieves 0.997±0.005. RoBERTa vanilla already succeeds (0.981±0.026) — hierarchy is flat. Scrambled=binary=9-class (all 0.997±0.005).\\n\\n**On ActorAttack (hard OOD)**: RoBERTa vanilla ALSO collapses (0.466±0.163), proving \\\"no DA needed\\\" was DD-specific. DeBERTa vanilla=0.258±0.000. Both encoders benefit from DA: DeBERTa 9-class=0.972±0.026, RoBERTa 9-class=0.939±0.046. Binary (0.990±0.008) ≈ 9-class (0.972±0.026) ≈ scrambled (0.951±0.043) — all three classification variants equivalent at Full (max gap 3.9pp, variance intervals fully overlap, n=3). Label quality is not a significant factor on ActorAttack, consistent with DD findings (D032).\\n\\n**On Human-Written Jailbreaks (exp8, ToxicChat, macro F1 — D036)**: Domain-adapted variants outperform vanilla on human-authored data from ToxicChat (LMSYS, 204 jailbreaks + 204 benign, balanced, no data leakage). Macro F1: JB-MLM=0.837±0.013, wiki-MLM=0.791±0.093, binary=0.705±0.072, scrambled=0.616±0.056, 9-class=0.596±0.087, topic=0.496±0.042, vanilla=0.413±0.021. All DA variants exceed vanilla (+18-42pp), confirming features learned from Qwen3-8B synthetic data transfer to human text. JB-MLM strongest on human data (reversed from synthetic where 9-class leads), suggesting pure domain exposure transfers better across the human/LLM style gap. Notably, wiki-MLM (0.791, out-of-domain text) outperforms ALL classification variants (binary=0.705, scrambled=0.616, 9-class=0.596), further demonstrating that MLM domain exposure mechanism dominates classification supervision on human data. The jb_mlm vs wiki_mlm gap (4.6pp, std overlap) is modest, suggesting domain specificity provides marginal benefit on human data. 9-class drops from best on synthetic OOD (0.997/0.972) to 5th on human data (0.596), indicating classification supervision overfits to synthetic data distribution. Paper must discuss this contrast explicitly. NOTE: verazuo/jailbreak_llms results INVALID (benign data leakage, M014) — removed from paper.\\n\\n**Cross-encoder CKA (D029)**: Both MLM and 9-class reshape representations substantially (centered CKA < 0.5 vs vanilla), but representation change magnitude ≠ detection improvement. RoBERTa MLM changes more (CKA=0.393) yet gains only 0.6pp; DeBERTa MLM changes less (CKA=0.442) yet gains 60pp. The starting point determines the return on adaptation.\\n\\n**Cross-LLM Generalization (D049/D051, exp16/exp16b/exp17)**: On mixed Qwen+Llama training data (exp16, 250+250, 3 seeds), the evidence hierarchy REVERSES to 9-class >> MLM ≈ vanilla. DD OOD: 9-class=0.818±0.096, MLM=0.289±0.101, vanilla=0.291±0.065. ToxicChat human data (D051 correction, balanced 408-sample eval): 9-class=0.550±0.052, vanilla=0.356±0.009 — NO improvement over Qwen-only (exp8 9-class=0.596±0.087, stds overlap). Mixed training eliminates single-LLM confound but does NOT improve human-data generalization. On Llama-only training (exp16b, seed 42): MLM ≤ vanilla on all OOD (Llama DD: MLM=0.191, vanilla=0.181; Llama AA: MLM=0.290, vanilla=0.411 — vanilla better by 12pp). Scaling to 1000 Qwen conversations (exp17, 3 seeds): 9-class maintains dominance on OOD (DD=0.889±0.041, AA=0.913±0.041) while MLM remains weak (DD=0.338±0.010, AA=0.351±0.018, ≈ vanilla). **Critical revision**: MLM effectiveness is specific to single-source (Qwen) training data; classification-based DAPT (9-class) is the ONLY robust adaptation across all training data compositions.\\n\\n**Key nuances**: (1) Binary ≈ 9-class ≈ scrambled on ActorAttack Full (0.990 vs 0.972 vs 0.951, all within noise at n=3; D032). Label quality is not a detection-relevant factor on any OOD difficulty. (2) Classification supervision consistently reduces cross-seed variance 4-20x (Claim 3): 9-class std=0.005-0.026 vs MLM std=0.080-0.115, across both DD and ActorAttack. (3) Perturbation robustness is attack-family-dependent (Discussion finding): DD OOD Δ≈0, ActorAttack DA variants Δ=-0.17 to -0.35 (vanilla -0.009 floor effect). DA detection advantage survives perturbation (McNemar p<0.001) as Claim 1 corollary. (4) **D049**: MLM's ~60pp improvement on DD OOD is Qwen-specific; on mixed-LLM and Llama-only data, MLM ≈ vanilla. 9-class is the only universally robust DAPT variant.\\n\\n## Novelty Positioning (D030 reframe)\\nNOT: \\\"Persuasion-aware fine-tuning is the critical ingredient\\\" (MLM control falsified this on DD).\\nNOT: \\\"RoBERTa doesn't need domain adaptation\\\" (ActorAttack falsified this — D030).\\nNOT: \\\"Label quality matters on hard attacks\\\" (3-seed ActorAttack: binary 0.990 ≈ 9-class 0.972 ≈ scrambled 0.951, all within noise; D032).\\nNOT: \\\"Perturbation robustness generalizes across attack families\\\" (exp6 falsified: DD Δ≈0 but ActorAttack Δ=-0.17 to -0.35 for all variants).\\nNOT: \\\"MLM domain exposure is the universal mechanism\\\" (D049: MLM effectiveness is Qwen-specific; on mixed-LLM and Llama-only data, MLM ≈ vanilla).\\nYES: \\\"We systematically decompose cross-attack generalization across two encoder architectures, three OOD attack families, and two training data compositions. The key finding: classification-based domain adaptation (9-class) is the only universally robust adaptation variant — it improves generalization regardless of encoder architecture, attack complexity, or training data source. MLM continued pretraining is effective on single-source data but collapses on mixed-LLM training, revealing its effectiveness is condition-dependent. Classification supervision provides additional benefits: cross-seed stability (std reduced 4-20x vs MLM on synthetic OOD) and interpretability via strategy trajectories. Mixed-LLM training eliminates the single-LLM confound without degrading performance (exp16 ToxicChat=0.550±0.052 ≈ exp8 0.596±0.087 on balanced eval). DA's detection advantage survives adversarial perturbation (McNemar p<0.001), though absolute perturbation robustness is attack-family-dependent. Label quality (correct vs scrambled vs binary) does not significantly affect detection at any OOD difficulty level (D032).\\\"\\n\\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources including real WildChat). TF-IDF remains strong on DD OOD (Full=0.961, per-prefix K1-K5 near-perfect). **But TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (Full=0.261, exp7)** — lexical features cannot capture multi-persona role-playing attack structure. DisPeD's value: (1) bridging 71pp gap vs TF-IDF on hard OOD (ActorAttack: 9-class=0.972 vs TF-IDF=0.261), (2) cross-seed stability (std 4-20x smaller than MLM variants), (3) interpretability via strategy trajectories.\\n\\n## Comprehensive Ablation Picture (D014, updated D019/D020, revised D049)\\nSystematic ablation reveals the method's actual mechanism:\\n- **Critical (conditioned, D049)**: On single-source Qwen data, domain exposure in any form (MLM, scrambled, persuasion) bridges OOD gap from F1=0.28 to F1≈1.0. **However**, on mixed-LLM (Qwen+Llama) and different-source (Llama-only) data, only classification-based DAPT (9-class) reliably bridges the gap; MLM effectiveness is condition-dependent (Qwen-specific, provides no benefit on mixed data: DD OOD=0.289±0.101 ≈ vanilla=0.291).\\n- **Harmful**: Misaligned classification objective (topic: F1=0.54±0.21, -37pp vs MLM) — constrains representations toward irrelevant features\\n- **Non-critical on single-source**: Label granularity (binary≈scrambled≈9-class, 1-6pp gap on DD), Lambda tuning (all F1=1.0), Temporal modeling (mean pooling≈GRU on DD)\\n- **Now critical on multi-source (D049)**: Classification supervision itself — 9-class is the ONLY variant that maintains performance across training data compositions. MLM collapses on mixed data (DD OOD: 0.289±0.101 vs 9-class 0.818±0.096).\\n- **Implication (revised D049)**: On single-source data, domain exposure is sufficient. On multi-source/mixed data, classification supervision becomes essential. 9-class taxonomy provides: (1) robust cross-composition generalization, (2) cross-seed stability, (3) interpretability. The original \\\"domain exposure is the mechanism\\\" claim holds only within single-source training conditions.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered — D019 reframe, D034 Claim 3 restructure)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Classification-Based DAPT Provides Robust Cross-Attack Generalization (D049 reframe)\\nVanilla DeBERTa catastrophically fails on unseen attack types (F1=0.28 on DD). Classification-based domain-adaptive pretraining (9-class) bridges this gap robustly across ALL tested conditions — single-source (Qwen DD=0.997), mixed-LLM (DD=0.818), expanded scale (DD=0.889, AA=0.913). On single-source Qwen data, MLM also achieves near-perfect DD OOD (0.914±0.115), but this effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed Qwen+Llama data, MLM provides no benefit (DD=0.289±0.101, ≈ vanilla 0.291); on Llama-only data, MLM ≤ vanilla (DD: 0.191 vs 0.181; AA: 0.290 vs 0.411). 9-class is the ONLY adaptation variant that reliably improves generalization regardless of training data composition. Complete evidence hierarchy (Qwen-only DD Full F1, multi-seed): vanilla (0.28) < topic (0.54±0.21) < wiki-MLM (0.90±0.12) ≈ JB-MLM (0.91±0.12) < TF-IDF (0.96) < scrambled (0.99) ≈ binary (0.99) ≪ 9-class (1.00±0.005). On ActorAttack: TF-IDF collapses (0.261) while 9-class=0.972 (+71pp). ToxicChat human data (D051, balanced 408-sample eval): exp16 mixed 9-class=0.550±0.052 ≈ exp8 Qwen-only 9-class=0.596±0.087 — mixed training does NOT improve human-data generalization but eliminates single-LLM confound.\\n- Evidence: plan_017 MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.914±0.115, K≤5 ≥0.97 (no classification labels)\\n- Evidence: plan_018 wiki-MLM DD — 3-seed Full=0.904±0.115, ≈ JB-MLM (domain specificity not significant at Full)\\n- Evidence: plan_016v2 topic DD — 3-seed 0.541±0.207 (misaligned classification hurts vs MLM)\\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD — Treatment F1=1.0, Baseline=0.28 (+72pp)\\n- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — F1=0.935-0.990 (labels non-essential)\\n- Evidence: plan_013 binary DD — F1=0.99 (label granularity non-essential)\\n- Evidence: exp6 perturbation — DA detection advantage survives adversarial perturbation (9-class perturbed ActorAttack=0.768 >> vanilla=0.248, McNemar p<0.001). Domain adaptation's generalization benefit is not erased by paraphrasing attacks.\\n- Evidence: exp7 TF-IDF ActorAttack — TF-IDF Full=0.261 vs 9-class=0.972 (+71pp). TF-IDF collapses on structurally complex attacks (Precision=1.0 but Recall=0.15), proving lexical features are insufficient. DD OOD TF-IDF per-prefix: K1=1.0, K2=1.0, K3=0.994, K5=1.0, Full=0.961 — DD is fully solvable by lexical methods, but ActorAttack is not.\\n- Evidence: exp8 Human data (ToxicChat, macro F1) — 204 jailbreaks + 204 benign (LMSYS): JB-MLM=0.837±0.013, 9-class=0.596±0.087, vanilla=0.413±0.021. 泛化：攻击模式可迁移，benign 分布是瓶颈 (2026-05-19, exp14+exp14b+exp14c)\\n- **exp14 (Llama all)**：所有 variant 崩溃至随机水平（9class 0.433, jb_mlm 0.282, vanilla 0.261）\\n- **exp14b (Llama JB + Qwen benign)**：9class 回升至 1.000 (+56.7pp), jb_mlm 回升至 0.778 (+49.6pp)\\n- **exp14c (confusion matrix)**：TP rate 完全一致（exp14 vs exp14b），FP rate Llama benign 88-100% vs Qwen benign 0%\\n- **结论**：DAPT 学到的攻击模式跨 LLM 可迁移，但 benign 分布覆盖不足导致高 FP。F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸\\n- **论文定位**：limitation 归为 \\\"benign distribution coverage\\\"（可解决的数据多样性问题），而非方法论缺陷\\n- **Future work**：多 LLM benign 数据扩增\\n\\n### ⚠️ Capacity-DAPT 交互效应 (2026-05-19, exp15+exp15b+exp15c)\\n- **exp15 发现**：RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla AA Full=1.000 > 9class 0.946。大模型容量足以直接解决 AA\\n- **exp15b 证伪 catastrophic forgetting**：LR=1e-5 两个数据集均更差（DD 0.948 vs 0.987, AA 0.781 vs 0.946）。不是超参问题\\n- **exp15c MLM-only DAPT 也 hurt**：MLM AA Full=0.874，比 9class 0.946 和 vanilla 1.000 都差。不是 classification head 的问题\\n- **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\\n\\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\\n\\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\\n\\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + RoBERTa-large）GRU 参数一致：hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, MLP 512→256→2\\n\\n### ⚠️ MLM checkpoint 格式不兼容 (2026-05-19, exp16+exp17)\\n- **问题**: `train_deberta_mlm.py` 用 HF `save_pretrained()` 保存为 safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\\\"model.pt\\\")` 加载\\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 `AutoModel.from_pretrained()` 原生支持 safetensors\\n- **教训**: exp16 和 exp17 MLM 均因此崩溃，每次都需手动修复。应修改 train_classifier.py 的 treatment mode 统一支持 safetensors\\n\\n### ⚠️ exp16 Multi-LLM 结果 (2026-05-19)\\n- **无数据泄漏**: conversation_id 和内容 MD5 均无 train/test 重叠\\n- **9-class F1=1.0 on mixed_test 是真实的**: 76 样本小测试集 + 强 DAPT embedding，BiGRU Epoch 1 即达 val_acc 0.9865\\n- **Train Loss=0.0 是过拟合不是泄漏**: BiGRU 在预计算 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 9-class，dd_ood .422, aa_ood .217, llama_aa .124\\n\\n### ⚠️ Frozen probing: DAPT 类型间表征质量差异小 (2026-05-20, exp26+exp36)\\n- **发现**：冻结 9-class (exp26) vs 冻结 MLM (exp36) 的 probing 性能差距仅 3.1pp AA OOD (0.972 vs 0.941)\\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length shortcut\\n- **机制**：AA 攻击信号更微妙（no explicit harmful content），大量 benign 尾部 turns 稀释 BiGRU attention，导致漏检\\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\\n\\n## API Key / 模型版本\\n（待记录）\\n\", \"total_chars\": 4874, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 attention，导致漏检\\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\\n\\n## API Key / 模型版本\\n（待记录）\\n\", The mechanistic interpretation needs revision since the claim about universal label irrelevance doesn't hold—it's more nuanced, with labels providing modest benefit specifically for ActorAttack but not the other families. Though I should consider whether this effect is being driven by an outlier seed rather than a genuine pattern.

Looking at the negative result protocol, this is a partial work situation—labels matter for the hardest OOD family but not for the easier ones. The hypothesis that n=10 would show equivalence on AA was falsified with p=0.183 and d=0.44, which actually tells me that correct labels do provide real benefit for the most challenging distribution shift. Comparing across experiments, the effect strengthens with more data rather than weakening, so the direction is consistent. I need to revise the paper claims to reflect that label relevance varies by task difficulty rather than claiming complete irrelevance.

The core question is whether this d=0.44 effect size shifts the narrative from "negligible" to "modest but meaningful" and whether that undermines the C3 claim about optimization regularization over semantic learning. The paper already hedges with "limited sensitivity" and the 3pp gap, but I should present both options to the Director: either update with the stronger n=10 evidence or stick with the more conservative n=5 results.

Option 2 would be cherry-picking data, so it's really between updating the paper with the new findings or reframing C3 to acknowledge a residual semantic contribution on hard OOD cases. I'll report this to the Director and then move on to setting up Experiment D, which involves mixing benign WildChat data into training to reduce the false positive rate from 68.1%.

Let me think about what this experiment involves:
1. Take WildChat benign conversations

3. Retrain the model

This connects to exp38_wildcat_calibration, which revealed the WildChat FPR issue is genuine domain shift rather than a calibration problem. Now I need to register Experiment D properly and set up a Worker to run it, but first I should reconcile what we know from prior experiments—exp38 showed AUC=0.70 on WildChat indicating real distribution mismatch, and exp14c demonstrated how easily false positives can explode.

The core idea for Experiment D is straightforward: if I mix WildChat benign examples into the training data alongside Qwen benign, the model should learn to recognize WildChat's benign distribution and dramatically reduce false positives on it while preserving jailbreak detection. I have the WildChat benign data from exp38 already available, though the 226 samples might be limiting—I may need to pull more. 检测性能。\n\n### 背景\n- 当前模型在 WildChat benign 上 FPR=68.1%（9-class 3-seed mean），原因是训练 benign 数据全部来自 Qwen-generated conversations，与真实用户对话分布不匹配\n- exp38 证实这是 genuine domain shift（AUC=0.70），不是 calibration 问题\n- exp14c 证实 F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸，jailbreak 检测本身是好的\n- 论文 Appendix 已报告 WildChat FPR=68.1% 作为 limitation\n\n### 环境\n- 服务器: westd-33183\n- GPU: cuda:0 (RTX PRO 6000 96GB)\n- Python: conda base /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/plan_002_splits/\n- WildChat benign 数据: /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/wildchat_benign_226.jsonl（可能存在，来自 exp38）\n\n### 执行步骤\n\n**Step 1: 了解现有数据和训练管线**\nSSH (read_only) 到 westd-33183：\n- `ls /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/` — 查看所有数据文件\n- 检查 wildchat_benign 数据是否存在及其格式（jsonl，每行一个 conversation）\n- 查看 plan_002_splits/ 中 train.jsonl 的格式和 benign 数量\n- 查看 Stage 1 训练脚本（train_deberta.py 或类似）的输入格式\n- 检查 train.jsonl 中 benign 的数量和格式\n\n**Step 2: 准备混合数据**\n策略：将 WildChat benign 对话添加到训练集的 benign 部分。\n- 如果 wildchat_benign_226.jsonl 存在（226 条），用其中约 150 条作为训练，76 条作为评估\n- 将 WildChat benign 训练部分（~150 条）转换为与 train.jsonl 相同的格式，label 标为 benign/none\n- 创建新的混合训练文件：原始 train.jsonl + WildChat benign 训练部分\n- 保持原始 validation set 不变\n\n如果 wildchat_benign_226.jsonl 不存在或数量不够：\n- 检查 /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/ 下有无其他 wildchat 数据文件\n- 可能需要从 HuggingFace 下载 WildChat 数据集并提取 benign conversations\n\n**Step 3: 训练**\n用混合数据训练 9-class 模型（3 seeds: 42, 123, 456）：\n- Stage 1: DeBERTa 9-class DAPT (lr=2e-5, batch_size=16, epochs=4)\n- Stage 2: BiGRU (20 epochs)\n- 参考现有训练脚本的用法，查看 exp39 或 plan_002 的训练命令\n- 用 `submit_training_job` 提交（不要 nohup）\n\n**Step 4: 评估**\n训练完成后评估：\n1. **WildChat FPR**：在 WildChat benign held-out（~76 条）上计算 FPR\n2. **OOD jailbreak detection**：DD OOD, AA OOD, FITD OOD 上的 F1 macro\n3. **IID 性能**：确认 IID 不退化\n\n对比 baseline（原始 plan_002 模型的 WildChat FPR=68.1%）。\n\n**Step 5: 更新 registry**\n- 转 RUNNING 时提供 spec\n- 完成后更新 metrics（WildChat FPR, OOD F1, IID F1）\n- 设置 checkpoint_path 和 log_path\n- conclusion: success 如果 FPR<30% 且 OOD F1>0.95；negative 如果 FPR 仍>30%\n\n### 注意事项\n- WildChat 数据可能有 conversation_id 字段，确保训练/测试不重叠\n- WildChat 中可能混有 jailbreak 内容（exp9 发现 14-16%），如果有 is_jailbreak/toxic 标签则过滤，否则需要简单过滤\n- 训练前检查磁盘空间（df -h），确保 >15GB 可用\n- 每个 DeBERTa checkpoint 约 735MB\n- 训练预计 30-60 分钟（3 seeds × Stage1 + Stage2）\n",
  "context": "exp42_ood_benign_mixed 已在 registry 中注册，status=PREPARING, hypothesis=\"Including WildChat benign conversations in training data will reduce WildChat FPR from 68.1% to below 30% while maintaining jailbreak OOD detection F1 above 0.95 on DD/AA/FITD\", parent=exp38_wildcat_calibration.\n\n关键背景：\n- 现有 WildChat 评估数据: 226 条 benign (wildchat_benign_226.jsonl)\n- 现有训练 benign: Qwen-generated, 全部在 plan_002_splits/train.jsonl 中\n- exp38 结果: AUC=0.70, FPR=79% (default threshold), calibration 无法解决\n- exp14c: FP explosion 是唯一的失败原因，jailbreak TP 完全正常"

限定为 synthetic。\n\n### D038: 实验阶段结束 (2026-05-19)\n- 当前证据足够，不再需要更多实验。进入论文修改收尾阶段。DDL 2026-05-25。\n\n## 实验经验\n\n### 磁盘空间约束 (2026-05-17, updated 2026-05-22)\n- westd-33183 根分区总容量 30GB。DeBERTa checkpoint ~735MB。\n- 旧 checkpoint（exp1/mf1/plan 系列）已迁移至 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints_archive/`\n- 新实验 checkpoint 输出到 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints/`\n- 2026-05-22: 清理 19 个旧 checkpoint 目录释放 ~19.6GB，当前约 21GB 可用\n\n### ⚠️ Single-seed 结论偏差警告 (2026-05-18)\n- GRU 随机性可导致 single-seed 偏差 25pp+，必须多 seed 验证\n\n### ⚠️ 跨 LLM 泛化：攻击模式可迁移，benign 分布是瓶颈 (2026-05-19, exp14+exp14b+exp14c)\n- **exp14 (Llama all)**：所有 variant 崩溃至随机水平（9class 0.433, jb_mlm 0.282, vanilla 0.261）\n- **exp14b (Llama JB + Qwen benign)**：9class 回升至 1.000 (+56.7pp), jb_mlm 回升至 0.778 (+49.6pp)\n- **exp14c (confusion matrix)**：TP rate 完全一致（exp14 vs exp14b），FP rate Llama benign 88-100% vs Qwen benign 0%\n- **结论**：DAPT 学到的攻击模式跨 LLM 可迁移，但 benign 分布覆盖不足导致高 FP。F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸\n- **论文定位**：limitation 归为 \"benign distribution coverage\"（可解决的数据多样性问题），而非方法论缺陷\n- **Future work**：多 LLM benign 数据扩增\n\n### ⚠️ Capacity-DAPT 交互效应 (2026-05-19, exp15+exp15b+exp15c)\n- **exp15 发现**：RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla AA Full=1.000 > 9class 0.946。大模型容量足以直接解决 AA\n- **exp15b 证伪 catastrophic forgetting**：LR=1e-5 两个数据集均更差（DD 0.948 vs 0.987, AA 0.781 vs 0.946）。不是超参问题\n- **exp15c MLM-only DAPT 也 hurt**：MLM AA Full=0.874，比 9class 0.946 和 vanilla 1.000 都差。不是 classification head 的问题\n- **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\n\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\n\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\n\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + RoBERTa-large）GRU 参数一致：hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, MLP 512→256→2\n\n### ⚠️ MLM checkpoint 格式不兼容 (2026-05-19, exp16+exp17)\n- **问题**: `train_deberta_mlm.py` 用 HF `save_pretrained()` 保存为 safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\"model.pt\")` 加载\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 `AutoModel.from_pretrained()` 原生支持 safetensors\n- **教训**: exp16 和 exp17 MLM 均因此崩溃，每次都需手动修复。应修改 train_classifier.py 的 treatment mode 统一支持 safetensors\n\n### ⚠️ exp16 Multi-LLM 结果 (2026-05-19)\n- **无数据泄漏**: conversation_id 和内容 MD5 均无 train/test 重叠\n- **9-class F1=1.0 on mixed_test 是真实的**: 76 样本小测试集 + 强 DAPT embedding，BiGRU Epoch 1 即达 val_acc 0.9865\n- **Train Loss=0.0 是过拟合不是泄漏**: BiGRU 在预计算 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 9-class，dd_ood .422, aa_ood .217, llama_aa .124\n\n### ⚠️ Frozen probing: DAPT 类型间表征质量差异小 (2026-05-20, exp26+exp36)\n- **发现**：冻结 9-class (exp26) vs 冻结 MLM (exp36) 的 probing 性能差距仅 3.1pp AA OOD (0.972 vs 0.941)\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\n\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length shortcut\n- **机制**：AA 攻击信号更微妙（no explicit harmful content），大量 benign 尾部 turns 稀释 BiGRU attention，导致漏检\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\n\n### ⚠️ TOST 10-seed: Label correctness IS family-dependent (2026-05-22, exp41)\n- **发现**：扩展到 n=10 seeds 后，DD/FITD 仍确认 label equivalence（TOST p≈0），但 AA 上非等价性更明显（p=0.183, d=0.44 medium effect）\n- d 从 n=5 的 0.09 升至 n=10 的 0.44——n=5 时的\"negligible\"是统计效力不足的假象\n- 9-class AA mean=0.979, scrambled AA mean=0.949, gap=3pp，但 scrambled 方差大（seed 314: 0.833 outlier）\n- **论文影响**：当前写法\"d=0.09 on ActorAttack\"需更新为 d=0.44 (n=10)。C3 \"optimization regularization, not semantic learning\" 对 DD/FITD 成立，但 AA 上 correct labels 提供 modest semantic benefit\n- **等 Director 决策后修改 paper**\n\n## API Key / 模型版本\n（待记录）\n"
}

exp41 关键发现：n=10 后 AA TOST 失败 (p=0.183, d=0.44 medium)，但 DD/FITD 仍 confirmed (p≈0)。这不是坏消息——条件性发现比 blanket null 更有价值。

   - DD: TOST confirmed (p=2e-9), d≈0 → label content negligible
   - FITD: TOST confirmed (p≈0), d≈0
   - AA: TOST inconclusive (p=0.183), d=0.44 (medium) → label content has moderate effect on structurally complex attacks
   - 删除 "negligible on both" 措辞，改为按攻击类型分别报告

- 这是更好的 story——解释了 WHY labels matter for some attacks but not others

### Tool result

_source line 517 · 2026-05-22T04:00:27.441Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Mb5sUxCtv2HZpJiZHCgEXB`</summary>

```text
1	% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.
4	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, and existing defenses fail to generalize across attack families: Llama Guard~3, a widely deployed turn-level safety classifier, detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation.
5	We isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.
6	On single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\,=\,0.818 vs.\ 0.291 without adaptation).
7	Across three architectures (125--355M), the set of attacks requiring adaptation shrinks with scale, though architecture and pretraining differences prevent isolating scale as the sole factor.
8	Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds), with limited sensitivity to label correctness, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.
9	These findings suggest classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, not semantic feature learning.
10	\end{abstract}
11	
```

### Tool result

_source line 518 · 2026-05-22T04:00:25.942Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01M2jJHDqdjZfLQ719rvuUpi`</summary>

```text
100	\vspace{-2pt}
101	{\footnotesize $^{*}$9-class encoder with mean pooling replacing BiGRU. $^{\dagger}$Deterministic (no std). $^{\ddagger}$MLM-only IID (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}) is single-seed (42); all OOD values are 3-seed. $^{\P}$Zero-shot; no domain training.}
102	\end{table}
103	
104	A clear \emph{evidence hierarchy} emerges across seven variants (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}), ordered by OOD detection at full conversation length on DD:
105	
106	\vspace{2pt}
107	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026) $<$ topic (0.541$\pm$0.207) $\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) $\approx$ jb-MLM (0.914$\pm$0.115) $<$ scrambled (0.994$\pm$0.005) $=$ 9-class (0.994$\pm$0.005) $\approx$ binary (1.000$\pm$0.000)}
108	\vspace{2pt}
109	
110	\paragraph{Domain exposure is the primary mechanism.}
111	MLM bridges $\sim$60\,pp from vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026 $\to$ 0.914$\pm$0.115) without labels; this supports the DAPT interpretation~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.
112	All three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.994--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \geq 0.40$) provide descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence. Extended to $n$=5 seeds, TOST (margin = 5\,pp) confirms equivalence on DD ($p < 0.001$; Table~\ref{tab:tost_sensitivity}); on ActorAttack, the mean gap narrows to 0.5\,pp with TOST approaching significance ($p$\,=\,0.08, SE\,=\,0.026). Cohen's $d$ for 9-class vs.\ scrambled is negligible on both DD ($d \approx 0$) and ActorAttack ($d$\,=\,0.09 at $n$=5, down from 0.59 at $n$=3; Table~\ref{tab:seed_level}). Label quality and granularity are non-factors for detection.
113	Classification supervision is associated with 4--20$\times$ lower cross-seed variance over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\ 0.115 on DD; observed range across 3 seeds; formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at $n$=3; cross-seed ensemble yields marginal additional gains, Appendix~\ref{app:ensemble}). This stability benefit does not transfer to human-authored data, where classification-supervised variants exhibit the highest variance and pure MLM the lowest (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
114	Appendix~\ref{app:tsne} provides t-SNE visualizations confirming that domain-adapted variants produce tighter, more separable embedding clusters than the vanilla baseline.
115	
116	\paragraph{Domain specificity is marginal at full length but meaningful for early detection.}
117	Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length (1\,pp gap), though jailbreak-domain text provides an 8\,pp advantage at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911).
118	Perturbation analysis (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}) reveals a sharper domain specificity effect: Wikipedia MLM degrades by $\sim$12\,pp under adversarial paraphrasing while jailbreak-domain MLM degrades by only 2.6\,pp.
119	
120	\paragraph{Misaligned supervision destabilizes generalization.}
121	Topic classification yields DD-Full F1 = 0.541$\pm$0.207; seed 123 falls \emph{below} vanilla (0.252 vs.\ 0.312). Despite using the same jailbreak-domain training data, topic classification performs worse than Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115), which uses out-of-domain text with no labels. A misaligned objective forces the encoder to allocate capacity to irrelevant features and actively destabilizes representations that MLM would preserve.
122	
123	\paragraph{Evidence hierarchy on structurally complex attacks.}
124	ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} uses multi-persona steering across longer conversation structures and provides a more challenging OOD benchmark than DD.
125	The evidence hierarchy at full conversation length is:
126	
127	\vspace{2pt}
128	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small TF-IDF (0.261) $\approx$ vanilla (0.258$\pm$0.000) $<$ topic (0.625$\pm$0.141) $<$ wiki-MLM (0.686$\pm$0.297) $<$ jb-MLM (0.893$\pm$0.080) $<$ scrambled (0.951$\pm$0.043) $\approx$ 9-class (0.972$\pm$0.026) $\approx$ binary (0.990$\pm$0.008)}
129	\vspace{2pt}
130	
131	TF-IDF collapses from 0.961 (DD) to 0.261 (ActorAttack, precision = 1.0, recall = 0.15). This gap shows that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond bag-of-words.
132	All classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent performance (0.951--0.990, max gap 3.9\,pp); this extends the DD finding. DAPT provides $\sim$71\,pp over both vanilla (0.258) and TF-IDF (0.261). Full per-prefix results and per-prefix error analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:actorattack_full} and~\ref{app:aa_error}.
133	
134	\paragraph{Temporal modeling.}
135	Mean pooling matches BiGRU on both DD and ActorAttack (F1 gap $\leq$ 1\,pp, overlapping CIs; Appendix~\ref{app:temporal}).
136	Turn-order shuffling produces negligible change ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) across both DeBERTa-v3-base 9-class (DD $\Delta$\,=\,0.000, AA $\Delta$\,=\,$-$0.009, IID $\Delta$\,=\,0.000) and RoBERTa-large 9-class (DD $\Delta$\,=\,+0.001, AA $\Delta$\,=\,$-$0.007, IID $\Delta$\,=\,0.000), confirming that detection relies on per-turn representations rather than sequential patterns.
137	
138	\paragraph{Summary.}
139	For base-size encoders (125--184M) on our synthetic multi-turn benchmark, domain exposure is the primary mechanism; label correctness, granularity, temporal modeling, and domain specificity are non-factors. Classification supervision provides a complementary stability benefit (variance reduced 4--20$\times$) without changing the detection ceiling.
140	DD and FITD confirm no regression on easier attacks; ActorAttack provides the discriminative evaluation where lexical methods fail.
141	Detection is stable across conversation lengths (Appendix~\ref{app:length}), near-saturates at 50 conversations (Appendix~\ref{app:data_efficiency}), and is insensitive to auxiliary loss weight (Appendix~\ref{app:lambda}).
142	
143	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
144	\subsection{Cross-Encoder Validation}
145	\label{sec:cross_encoder}
146	
147	\begin{table}[t]
148	\centering
149	\small
```

```text
1	% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	
5	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.
6	
7	Existing defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}
8	
9	Pretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\ DeepContext) drops from F1\,=\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.
10	
11	Our pipeline, \textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants systematically vary training objectives while sharing the same two-stage pipeline (Section~\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.
12	
13	\begin{figure}[t]
14	\centering
15	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}
16	\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}
17	\label{fig:architecture}
18	\end{figure}
19	
20	Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.289 $\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
21	
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Systematic decomposition identifying the most robust adaptation and three supplementary negative results.} Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations on synthetic multi-turn data shows classification-based DAPT is the most robust variant; unsupervised pretraining is effective on single-source data but collapses on mixed-LLM training. We report three supplementary findings that challenge common assumptions: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels (TOST-confirmed on DD at $n$\,=\,5; $d$\,=\,0.09 on ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant ($\Delta < 1$\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Architecture- and scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation.} Three encoder scales (125M, 184M, 355M) suggest adaptation need shrinks with model size, though encoders differ in architecture; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate scale. RoBERTa-large handles structurally complex attacks without adaptation (Section~\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).
27	
28	  \item \textbf{Mechanistic interpretation: optimization regularization, not semantic learning.} Three null results converge: frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp), label content irrelevance ($d$\,=\,0.09), and temporal modeling irrelevance ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) are consistent with classification supervision providing robustness through optimization regularization that stabilizes downstream training. This benefit is condition-dependent (variance reversal on human data) but classification DAPT remains strongest under mixed-LLM training (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). Continued pretraining is 266$\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:data_efficiency}).
29	\end{itemize}
30	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011VKNtx3d39RFebZMgBSjjQ`</summary>

```text
1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97).
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level.
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}
13	Classification supervision is associated with lower variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\times$ observed range, 3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013). Frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Encoder capacity and lexical analysis.}
16	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD).
17	
18	\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}
19	Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp), label irrelevance ($d$\,=\,0.09), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, not semantic feature learning, paralleling self-supervised results where training structure matters more than label content~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. Unlike random-label memorization~\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, random-label \emph{pretraining} here produces transferable representations, suggesting that classification structure regularizes the space. Three extensions emerge: MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training despite single-LLM effectiveness, frozen probing near-parity coexists with 8\,pp end-to-end gaps, and the hierarchy reverses on human data.
20	
21	\paragraph{Benign distribution as the remaining bottleneck.}
22	OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (benign is IID). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}); cross-LLM error decomposition confirms collapse is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text.
23	
24	\section{Conclusion}
25	
26	On synthetic multi-turn evaluations, classification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders, providing robustness through optimization regularization rather than semantic learning; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. Negative results on label correctness (TOST-confirmed on DD at $n$\,=\,5; $d$\,=\,0.09 on ActorAttack), temporal order, and domain specificity constrain which properties matter; continued pretraining is 266$\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).
27	
28	\section{Limitations}
29	\label{sec:limitations}
30	
31	All multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family). ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}).
32	
33	OOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}); residual calibration error (ECE\,=\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.
34	
35	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.
36	
37	\section*{Ethics Statement}
38	
39	This work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.
40	
```
